Let's design a versatile h-searcher:
- Choose a set of dimensions k: [2,..., maybe 200?] and also length 5582. Because direct O(k²) for k 5000 maybe okay if limited. But h optimization with local search for k=2000 might be hard.
- For each k:
   * Generate initial h from parametric families:
       1. Uniform.
       2. Two-block: [a repeated block?] But for point sequence h, use area distributions: e.g., h = [w,...] with two plateau blocks of lengths L1,L2? That corresponds to continuous block with variable widths, not just point lengths. We can also generate block-constant base: choose a coarse pattern of length m (continuum) and upscale to k. But h itself can have repeated values. We can generate h = smooth function f(i/k) clipped positive: sine/cosine, parabolas, exponentials, two slab with alpha phases, etc.
       3. Deterministic low-discrepancy ±1 mapping: h_i=(1+α s_i)/k with mean0, choose s as Thue-Morse, alternating, random, chirp; optimize α.
       4. Random Dirichlet, random Gaussian positive.
   * Run local optimization on h:

Thus we can implement optimization over h as a nonnegative density using parametric spectral coefficients:

Score function for h length k normalized sum1:
- q = convolve(h,h)
- score = 2*k*max(q) (if sum 1). Since if h not sum1, score =2*k*max(q)/(sum²). We'll maintain sum1.
- We can compute q via np.convolve for k up to maybe 2000; for k larger use FFT. But direct simpler. Need ensure nonnegative and no underflow.

Let's outline code in detail.

Global constants:
- Use time budget 2400s. Leave 10s for final evaluation.
- `evaluate_sequence` available but we can use internal score for speed. Internal score must match evaluator exactly except clipping/normalization. Our sequences will be finite nonnegative <1000, sum > .01. Use float64.
- Implement `direct_score(seq)` for list/np:
   n=len; if n==0 inf; sum; if sum<0.01 inf; compute conv = np.convolve(a,a) for n≤? else fft. Use score = 2*n*max(conv)/sum². For normalized h sum1, sum1.
- For many h updates, maintain normalized sum1 and nonnegative. But final sequence may need values not too small? Evaluator okay with tiny.

Helper `convolve_double(a):`
   if n <= 400? Use np.convolve direct. Else use scipy.fft.rfft? Since allowed numpy; we can implement:
      size = 1 << ((2*n-1).bit_length()) maybe power of two for speed. F=np.fft.rfft(a, size); c=np.fft.irfft(F*F, size)[:2*n-1]; clip small negative zero.
   This is O(n log n). For h many small, direct maybe faster. We can use threshold 300.

Helper `score_normalized(a):` assuming a nonnegative, maybe not sum1? Compute sum, if <.01; q=conv; return 2*n*max(q)/(sum*sum). For local search, we can avoid sum² by normalizing to sum1 and score=2*n*max(q). But for final ensure exact.

Helper `normalize(a)`: c=np.clip(a,0,None); s=c.sum(); if s>0 return c/s else uniform.

- uniform

Local improvement per candidate:
Function `improve_sequence(h, time_limit, rng, beta_schedule)`. It returns best h and score.
Implementation details:
- Convert to log domain? To maintain exact sum and nonnegative, we can update in logh.
- Initial logh = log(h + eps), subtract max for stability.
- Maintain best_h and best_score.
- Iteration loop while time < limit:
   - Occasional random restart within local basin: If no improvement for `stagnation` iterations, perturb logh by Gaussian std scaling (e.g., 0.1-0.3) and normalize, but keep best separate.
   - Choose step size: We can try a fixed natural-gradient step with multiple candidates generated by different eta/beta, evaluate, accept best improvement.
       For a set of params `(eta, beta, tau)`, compute update and score; choose if score < current. Since multiple evals per iter cost; but can be worthwhile. Maybe limit 2-3 variants.
   - Simpler: fixed beta=2, eta=0.3 adaptive. If no improvement for many, reduce step or increase beta (focus peaks), or random perturbation.
- Need compute gradient efficiently. For h vector length k, compute q=convolve(h,h). M=max. Weights `w = np.power(np.clip(q, 0, None), beta)` maybe. If sum w huge? Scale g = correlation(w, h) (length k). We want h_i decreases where g high. But note g_i dimension ~ q*h, h~1/k. The total gradient scales maybe 1/k. We can scale by current update magnitude.
   Natural gradient update: logh_new = logh - eta * g / g_scale, where g_scale = max(g) or 2*mean of quantile 90. Then h_temp = exp(logh_new - max) clip min, normalize.
   Potential issue: Multiplying gradient by all h including zeros; if h_i=0, g_i may be positive; exp(-inf) zero; okay. But if logh initialized with floor, h_i small. We can add small floor.
   If using normalized h directly, gradient of max relative to sum maybe should include normalization. Natural gradient on simplex: update logh handles normalization automatically.
   We can also apply a small reverse update to some variables to explore: `logh += eta * (g.mean() - g)/scale`? But steep.
- Since objective scale invariant, after update normalize.
- We can improve by active set coordinate: Identify lag d_max; for pairs (i,d-i) contributing, push down one of them (maybe the smaller or random). But gradient does that.

Let's add a deterministic **alternating projection / active smoothing** that can reduce peaks significantly from any start. Suppose we focus on top peak lag d*. To reduce q_d, the gradient is h_{d-i}. Multiplying down variables that pair at d. But if the peak is from self of a block (d=2a), gradient is h at positions within block, reducing the whole block. Could also use direct **peel**: when q_d max, for all pairs (i,j) with i+j=d, reduce the smaller of h_i,h_j? Or transfer mass from a variable to a less active variable. Targeted moves can reduce peaks.

Coordinate/block line search: For a peak lag d, variable h_i appears in q_d with counterpart h_{d-i}. Reducing h_i reduces q_d and other lags. We can try moving mass from a high variable to a low variable. Pair (i, j) maybe. Random mutation: choose two indices, redistribute mass. Since score scale invariant, we can do simplex moves. This is like simplex local search; dimension high but random. With 2400s for k=100, many moves.

Let's design a local search for small/medium k using **random simplex moves**:
- Start h normalized nonnegative.
- At each iteration, choose two indices i,j. Move mass: h_i' = h_i + δ h_j, h_j'=h_j - δ/(1+δ) h_j? Simpler: h_i*= (1+ε), h_j*=(1-ε) then normalize? Multiplicative pair.
- Or choose i donor, j receiver with h_j<h_i, transfer τ h_i.

def continuous_to_h(fgrid, G, k):
    # fgrid normalized area sum(f)=G.
    # sample k midpoints using linear interpolation of fgrid onto [0,1]
    x_grid = (np.arange(G)+0.5)/G
    x_sample = (np.arange(k)+0.5)/k
    f_samples = np.interp(x_sample, x_grid, fgrid)
    h = np.clip(f_samples, 0, None)
    h -= h.sum()/k? Wait to make point areas, h_i should be f_i * dx where dx=1/k? For score_h=2k max conv(h). If f is continuous, the discrete probability point masses h_i ≈ f(x_i) * dx = f/k. So set h = f_samples / k, then normalize sum1 (sum f_samples/k≈1). Equivalent h = f_samples; h /= k*h.sum? If f area normalized, sum f_samples ≈ k (since average f * k? Actually dx=1/k, sum f_samples/k ≈1 => sum f_samples ≈ k. Thus h = f_samples/k has sum1). So h = np.clip(f_samples,0,None); s=h.sum(); if s>0 h = h/(k? wait h_points = f_samples/k; sum h_points = sum f_samples/k ≈1. If sum f_samples not exactly k due discretization, normalize: h = f_samples/k; h /= h.sum(). That yields h_i ≈ f/k.
```

Local gradient function for normalized h:
```
def gradient_step(h, logh, eta, power, rng):
   q = conv_double(h); M = q.max(); if not finite: return...
   # weights
   if power>0: w = np.power(np.clip(q,0,None), power)
   else: w = (q >= (1-trend)*M)
   # normalize weights? Let's scale to mean 1 maybe.
   if w sum ==0: return
   smooth = w / w.sum()
   # correlation: r_i = sum_d w_d h[d-i]
   # Use direct formula with FFT or np.correlate?
   r = cross_corr_weights_h(smooth, h) # returns length k
   # g = 2*r
   scale = max(r) or mean positive
   new_logh = logh - eta * r / scale
   # maybe shrink range to avoid under/overflow; but exp handles with max subtract
   new_h = np.exp(new_logh - max(new_logh))
   # floor? If zeros, exp may underflow; add small to logh initially. If any 0, keep.
   new_h = np.clip(new_h,0,None)
   s=new_h.sum(); if s==0 fail; new_h/=s
   return new_h
```
But gradient of sum of weighted q: The objective F=∑w_d q_d? If w smooth independent, gradient g=2r. Decreasing h by g reduces F. But if eta too large, can oscillate. We can generate multiple eta, power, choose best.
Important: r_i = ∑ w_d h[d-i]. If w normalized sum1, r_i has scale h ~1/k times active fraction. The update logh - eta*r/scale; if scale=max, eta~1 okay.
However, if h_i small and r_i=0, no change; if h_i huge and paired, decrease.
Normalization in logh: We maintain logh = log(h)+c. After new_h normalized, update logh = log(new_h) (with floor). We can add small epsilon to logh to avoid zeros: `logh = np.log(np.maximum(h, 1e-12))` after each accept.

Pair move local search for small k:
```
def pair_move_improve(h, n_iter, rng):
  for ...:
    i = rng.integers(k); j = rng.integers(k); if same continue
    # choose donor with larger h perhaps
    if h[j] > h[i]: swap i,j
    # try move fraction tau of h_i to j
    for tau in [0.05,0.2,0.5,0.8]:
       newh = h.copy(); amt=tau*h[i]; newh[i]-=amt; newh[j]+=amt; normalize; score; accept if improve.
```
This can find discrete opt for k small. We can run for k up to 200 with ~10000 iterations. Use `np.convolve` fast. For k=500, 10000 moves *250k ops=2.5e9 maybe slow; limit moves based on k: `max_eval = 20000000 / (k*k)`? Actually target total ops. We'll allocate time.

Function `random_block_init(k, m, rng)`:
   boundaries = sorted rng.integers(0,k+1) unique; append0,k; block lengths >0.
   areas = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(m)*alpha) (alpha 0.5-2).
   h = zeros(k); for each block, h[start:stop] = area / length.
   return h (sum1). This directly represents a continuous slab. For equal widths m. We can sample m 2..10, alpha varying. This provides initial shapes.

Local search details:
```
def local_improve(h, deadline, rng, verbose=False):
    h = normalize(h)
    current = h.copy(); best=current.copy(); best_score = score_norm1(current)
    current_score=best_score
    logh = np.log(np.maximum(current, 1e-12))
    no_imp=0
    eta=0.5
    power=3.0
    while now<deadline:
       # compute q for current? We need for gradient and score. If current changed since last, compute.
       q = conv_double(current); M=q.max(); if not finite: break
       if M==0: break
       # weights maybe (q/M)^power
       qn = np.clip(q / M, 0, 1)
       w = qn ** power
       # Optional: if using top only, w = (qn > 0.7).astype(float); but power large.
       if w.sum()<=0: break
       w = w / w.sum()
       r = cross_corr(w, current) # length k
       # scale r robust
       rp = r[r>0]
       scale = np.percentile(rp, 90) if rp.size else 0
       if scale <=0: scale = np.max(r)
       newh = natural_update(logh, r, scale, eta)
       # try if exact better than current
       score_new = score_norm1(newh)
       if finite and score_new < current_score - 1e-12:
          current = newh; current_score=score_new; logh=log(max(current,eps))
          eta *= 1.05
          no_imp=0
          if current_score < best_score: best=current.copy(); best_score=current_score
       elif finite and score_new < best_score - 1e-12: # accept as best but keep current? maybe accept
          current = newh; current_score=score_new; ...
       else:
          no_imp +=1
          if no_imp % 20 ==0:
             eta = max(0.05, eta*0.8)
          if no_imp > 80:
             # perturb logh
             noise_std = max(0.02, 1.0/np.sqrt(k) * 2?)
             logh = logh + rng.normal(0, 1.0/np.sqrt(k), size=k)  # maybe small
             current = exp normalize(logh); current_score=score_norm1(current)
             if current_score > best_score*1.5: # bad perturbation
                logh = log(max(best,eps)); current=best; current_score=best_score
             no_imp=0; eta=0.5
       # Sometimes change power to focus less/more
       if no_imp > 30: power = min(16, power*1.2)
       if no_imp > 60: power = max(1, power*0.8)
    # final block polish
    return best
```
Noise std: h values ~1/k; logh perturb should be on log scale, std maybe 0.2? If k=100, 1/sqrt(k)=.1. We can use 0.2. But ensure not all zeros.

`natural_update`: `new_logh = logh - eta * r / scale;` Since we maintain logh normalized to max? It's okay. To avoid extreme negative, `new_logh = new_logh - np.max(new_logh)`; exp; add tiny floor? `new_h = np.exp(new_logh);` If some underflow to 0, okay. Normalize. Then logh=log(max(new_h,1e-12)). If all zeros (unlikely), return uniform.

Cross-correlation `r` via FFT:
```
def corr_weights_h(w, h):
    n = len(h)
    # Need r[i] = sum_{j=0}^{n-1} w[i+j] h[j]
    L = 1 << (len(w)+n-1).bit_length()
    FW = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    FH = np.fft.rfft(h, L)
    out = np.fft.irfft(FW * np.conj(FH), L)
    return out[:n]
```
Check with small n: w length3, h length2? For h length n=2, w length3. r0=w0h0+w1h1. r1=w1h0+w2h1. FFT formula with out[lag] maybe yes. If `FW*conj(FH)` gives sum w[k]h[k-lag]? Need verify in code? We can't run. Let's derive: DFT linear: ifft(FFT(w)*conj(FFT(h)))[m] = ∑_{t} w[t] h[t-m] (circular). With zero padding length L, for m=0: ∑ w[t]h[t] = w0h0+w1h1 (since h zero other). For m=1: ∑ w[t] h[t-1] = t=1 w1h0 + t=2 w2h1 = desired r1. Good. So `out[:n]` correct.
For n small direct maybe faster but okay.

`conv_double`:
```
def conv_double(a):
   n=len(a)
   if n <= 350:
      c=np.convolve(a,a)
   else:
      N=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
      F=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
      c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)[:2*n-1]
   # clip small negative
   c[ c < 0 & c > -1e-12] =0? Use np.maximum(c,0)
   return c
```
For normalized h values ~1/n, conv ~1/n; using FFT fine.

Score normalized: `max(conv)` maybe small. If `M<=0`, score 0? But sum1, conv positive.

Pair move polish function for small k:
```
def pair_improve(h, max_iters, rng):
   h=normalize; best=h; best_score=score
   for it in range(max_iters):
       i = rng.integers(n); j = rng.integers(n)
       if i==j: continue
       # try several transfer fractions from i to j and j to i
       # If h[i] > h[j], main move from i to j.
       for (d,r) in [(i,j),(j,i)]:
          if best[d] <=0: continue
          for tau in (0.03,0.1,0.3,0.6):
              nh=h.copy(); amt=tau*nh[d]; if amt>=nh[d]: amt=nh[d]*0.5; nh[d]-=amt; nh[r]+=amt; nh/=nh.sum(); score=score_norm1(nh); if score<best_score: accept to best and h=nh; break loops? 
   return best
```
Cost high; limit max_iters based on time. We can run if k<=200 for 5 seconds.

def block_improve(h, iterations, rng):

    width = max(1, int(rng.integers(1, max(2,k//8))))
    start = ...
    factor = exp(rng.normal(0,0.2))
    nh = h; nh[start:start+width]*=factor; normalize; score; accept.
```

- sine: s_i = sin(2π m i/k + φ) not ±? But for (1+α s) positive need |s|≤1; use continuous sine.
Then h= (1+α s) normalized.

This family could find good oscillating f. Combined with gradient/local.

Implementation specifics:

`_convolve_self(a)`:
```
n = a.size
if n <= 350:
    c = np.convolve(a, a)
else:
    size = 1
    while size < 2*n-1: size <<=1
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F*F, size)
    c = c[:2*n-1]
if c.size:
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
return c
```

`_score_unit(a)`:
```
if a.size==0: return inf
s=a.sum()
if s <=0: return inf
if abs(s-1)>1e-8: a = a/s # but maybe cost; for search ensure normalized.
c=_convolve_self(a)
m=c.max()
return float(2*a.size*m) # if sum exactly 1. If we normalized by scaling, sum maybe not1; use s: return 2*n*m/(s*s)
```
Use full formula for safety:
`return float(2*n*m/(s*s))`.

`_score_raw(a)` same but clip finite.

`_natural_update`:
```
def _natural_update(logh, r, scale, eta):
    new_logh = logh - (eta * r) / (scale if scale>0 else 1.0)
    new_logh = new_logh - np.max(new_logh)
    new_h = np.exp(new_logh)
    # ensure at least some mass; if sum <=1e-300, return uniform
    s = new_h.sum()
    if s <=0: return np.full_like(new_h, 1.0/new_h.size)
    new_h /= s
    return new_h
```

`_local_gradient_improve`:
```
def _local_gradient_improve(h, deadline, rng):
    h=_normalize(h)
    n=h.size
    current=h.copy(); current_score=_score_unit(current)
    best=current.copy(); best_score=current_score
    logh=np.log(np.maximum(current,1e-12))
    eta=0.4; power=3.0; no=0; allowed_power = ...
    while _now() < deadline:
       q = _convolve_self(current); M=q.max()
       if not finite or M<=0: break
       qn = np.clip(q/M,0,1)
       w = qn ** power
       sw=w.sum()
       if sw<=0: break
       w /= sw
       r = _corr_wh(w, current)
       rp = r[r>0]
       if rp.size:
          scale = float(np.percentile(rp, 75))
          if scale <=0: scale = float(np.max(rp))
       else: scale=1.0
       newh = _natural_update(logh, r, scale, eta)
       ns = _score_unit(newh)
       if finite and ns < current_score:
          current=newh; current_score=ns; logh=np.log(max(current,1e-12)); eta=min(2.0, eta*1.03); no=0
          if ns < best_score:
             best=current.copy(); best_score=ns
       else:
          no += 1
          if no % 10 == 0:
             eta = max(0.02, eta*0.85)
          if no > 80:
             # perturb current from best, but maintain some mass
             logh = np.log(np.maximum(best,1e-12))
             # random noise; std maybe 1.0/(2*sqrt(n))
             noise_std = 0.25 / math.sqrt(max(2.0, n / 100.0))? simpler 0.2
             logh = logh + rng.normal(0.0, 0.2, size=n)
             current=_natural_update(logh, r, max(scale,1e-12), 0.0) # no gradient? need normalize
             current_score=_score_unit(current)
             # If perturbation too bad, reset to best
             if not finite or current_score > best_score * 1.5:
                current=best.copy(); current_score=best_score; logh=np.log(max(current,1e-12))
             no=0; eta=0.4
          if no > 30:
             power = min(16.0, power*1.25)
          if no > 60:
             power = max(1.0, power*0.8)
    # after loop, maybe a few block perturb
    return best, best_score
```
Note `_natural_update` with eta=0 returns normalized exp(logh), okay.
For `power` change, if no_improve small, keep. If power too high, update may be sparse.

`_block_perturb_improve`: similar to previous but normalized. We'll use after gradient or as alternate. For each loop:
```
width = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n//16))) maybe scale with k
start = rng.integers(0, n-width)
factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.15)))
newh = current.copy(); newh[start:start+width] *= factor; normalize; score; if better accept. If no improve >100, increase std or split block? 
```
Can accept relative small. Use time.

`_pair_improve`: for k <= 400 maybe. Use time. It modifies current; evaluate score. But copying k array for each fractional move expensive. For pair move, we can update two elements in place and restore.
```
for iters:
  i,j chosen. amt=tau*h[i]; h[i]-=amt; h[j]+=amt; s? after transfer sum unchanged; score; if accept keep else restore.
```
No need normalize (sum same). Ensure nonnegative. For fractional try from donor to receiver; also reverse.
But if we keep best separately and current, use current copy.

- Random blocks for m in [2,3,4,5,6,8,12] repeated maybe 2 each. But generating many arrays. Keep list length limited (e.g., up to 20) to avoid too many local runs. For each, random parameters.
   For m: widths uniform or Dirichlet; areas Dirichlet(alpha). `area_alpha` random [0.5,1,2,5]. block lengths: sort random integers? Better to approximate continuous widths: boundaries quantiles from uniform/Dirichlet widths.    For widths uniform: use `np.linspace` with random jitter? We'll use random boundaries.
- Fourier: terms=1..3, random coefficients amplitude [0.2,0.5,0.8,1.0], frequency m from 1..4 random, phase random. `_fourier_init` computes f=1 + amplitude * sum cos; clip min1e-3, normalize. If all bad, fallback.

`_resample_sequence(seq, n)`:
- If seq is None or len 0 return None.
- a = np.asarray(seq); if any nonfinite, clean.
- Normalize.
- If a.size == n return a.
- If a.size > n: If a.size % n ==0: reshape(n, -1) mean axis1. Else use weighted averaging: x_old=np.linspace(0,1,a.size); x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n); interpolate? For downsampling, linear interp may create frequencies; okay. For mean pooling, use `np.interp` returns value at new points, not area average. Use `np.interp` for shape.
- If a.size < n: linear interp.
- After interp, clip >=0, normalize.
Use `np.interp` with endpoints 0,1. If seq length 1, fill.

`_upscale_h(h, n)`:
- h normalized. If n <= h.size: use downsample? For final candidates, n>=k mostly. If n < k, just return `_resample_sequence(h, n)`.
- k=h.size; base=n//k; extras=n%k; if base==0: return resample.
- out=zeros(n); pos=0
- for i in range(k): l=base+(1 if i<extras else0); val=h[i]/l if l>0 else0; out[pos:pos+l]=val; pos+=l
- Due exact shape, sum close1; normalize to be safe.

h=np.empty(k); h[:L]=left_area/L; h[L:]= (1-left_area)/R; normalize
```

`_random_block_init` implementation:
```
def _random_block_init(k, m, alpha, rng):
    if k<=0: return None
    if m >= k: return _normalize(rng.dirichlet(np.ones(k)*alpha))
    # boundaries: choose m unique sorted
    cuts = np.sort(rng.integers(1, k, size=m-1))
    cuts = np.concatenate(([0], cuts, [k]))
    # if duplicates, force unique by simple loop
    unique=[]; last=-1; for c in cuts: if c>last: unique.append(c); last=c else: maybe skip. Need ensure length m; if not fallback uniform cuts.
    areas = rng.dirichlet(np.full(m, alpha))
    h = zeros(k)
    for j in range(m):
       start=unique[j]; stop=unique[j+1]; l=stop-start
       if l>0: h[start:stop]=areas[j]/l
    return _normalize(h)
```
If many duplicates for small k, fallback. Ensure top-level no closure.

`_fourier_init`:
```
def _fourier_init(k, terms, rng, maxamp=1.0):
   if k<=0: return None
   x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, k)
   f = np.ones(k)
   for t in range(terms):
      freq = 1 + int(rng.integers(0, 4))  # 1-4
      phase = rng.uniform(0.0, 2*math.pi)
      amp = rng.uniform(0.15, maxamp)
      # maybe sign
      f += amp * np.cos(2*math.pi*freq*x + phase)
   f = np.maximum(f, 1e-4)
   return _normalize(f)
```
Need `math` imported.

`_exponential_init(k, beta, reverse=False)`.

`_structured_init`:
```
def _structured_init(k, kind, amp, rng):
    if k==1: return np.array([1.])
    if kind=='tm':
       t=np.zeros(k,int); s=np.ones(k); # t parity
       for i in range(1,k): t[i]=(t[i>>1]) ^ (i & 1)
       s = 1.0 - 2.0*t # if t=1 -> -1
    elif kind=='rs':
       seq=np.array([1,-1]) etc
       s=seq[:k]
       # ensure length by truncating; if truncated mean not zero, okay.
    elif kind=='chirp':
       x=np.arange(k)
       s = np.where(np.sin(2*math.pi * x*(x+1)/(2*k) + rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)) >=0, 1.0, -1.0)
    elif kind=='sin':
       s=np.sin(2*np.pi*rng.uniform(1,3)*x + phase)
    # combine maybe with random shift
    h = 1.0 + amp*s
    if kind in ['tm','rs','chirp'] and amp>1: amp=1
    h = np.maximum(h, 1e-6)
    return _normalize(h)
```
For `rs`, ensure mean maybe not zero if truncated; normalize okay but h mean not1/k; nonneg if amp≤1? If mean not zero, 1+amp*s might negative for negative s if amp≤1 no. Good.

`_resample_sequence`: Need if parent length huge. Use `np.interp`. For downsampling, linear interp may create negative? clip. For mean-pool, maybe better. But okay.
```
def _resample_sequence(seq, n):
    a=np.asarray(seq,float)
    if a.ndim!=1 or a.size==0: return None
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0); a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    a=_normalize(a)
    if a.size==n: return a
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,a.size)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    b=np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    b=np.clip(b,0,None)
    return _normalize(b)
```
For downsample, linear interpolation preserves shape but not area? Normalization okay.

def _pair_improve(h, deadline, rng):

   tau list [0.05,0.15,0.3,0.5]
   while now<deadline:
     i,j = rng.integers(n) twice; if same continue
     # donor current larger
     if current[i] >= current[j]: d=i,r=j else d=j,r=i
     moved=False
     for tau in taus:
        amt=tau*current[d]
        if amt<=0: continue
        current[d]-=amt; current[r]+=amt
        score=_score_unit(current) # sum unchanged, okay
        if finite and score<best_score:
           best=current.copy(); best_score=score; current_score=score; moved=True; no=0; break
        else:
           current[r]-=amt; current[d]+=amt
     if not moved: no++
     if no>200: reset current=best; no=0
   return best,best_score
```

Let's now produce final code. Ensure code only in python fence.

```python
"""
Searches for nonnegative step heights minimizing

    2 * n * max(convolve(a, a)) / (sum(a)**2)

The optimizer works on normalized probability vectors.  It uses a portfolio of
structured initializations (two-slab, slab, sinusoids, low-autocorrelation
sequences, exponents) followed by:
  * natural-gradient / multiplicative active-peak descent,
  * random block multiplicative polish,
  * tiny pair-transfer polish for small lengths.
Afterwards the best probability vector is upscaled to several lengths,
including a 5582-length version, and the best exact candidate is returned.
"""

import time
import math
import numpy as np


def _now():
    return time.monotonic()


def _clean_array(a):
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.ndim != 1 or arr.size == 0:
        return None
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return arr


def _normalize(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def _convolve_self(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    m = 2 * n - 1
    if n <= 320:
        c = np.convolve(a, a)
    else:
        L = 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
        f = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
        c = np.fft.irfft(f * f, L)[:m]
    if c.size == 0:
        return c
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def _score_raw(a):
    a = _clean_array(a)
    if a is None:
        return float("inf")
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        return float("inf")
    c = _convolve_self(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    mx = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx < 0.0:
        return float("inf")
    val = float(2.0 * n * mx / (s * s))
    return val if np.isfinite(val) else float("inf")


def _score_unit(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return float("inf")
    if abs(s - 1.0) > 1e-12:
        a = a / s
    c = _convolve_self(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    mx = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return float("inf")
    val = float(2.0 * a.size * mx)
    return val if np.isfinite(val) else float("inf")


def _corr_weights_h(w, h):
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64)
    n = h.size
    m = w.size + n - 1
    L = 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    fh = np.fft.rfft(h, L)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fw * np.conj(fh), L)
    return out[:n]


def _natural_update(logh, r, scale, eta):
    if scale <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(scale):
        scale = 1.0
    new_logh = logh - (eta * r) / scale
    mx = float(np.max(new_logh))
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return _normalize(np.maximum(np.exp(logh), 0.0))
    new_logh = new_logh - mx
    new_h = np.exp(new_logh)
    s = float(new_h.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(logh.size, 1.0 / logh.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return new_h / s


def _log_normalize(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64)
    h = np.maximum(h, 1e-15)
    return np.log(h / h.sum())


def _two_slab(k, left_frac=0.5, left_area=2.0 / 3.0):
    k = int(k)
    if k <= 1:
        return np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64)
    left_area = min(1.0, max(0.0, float(left_area)))
    L = int(round(k * left_frac))
    L = min(k, max(1, L))
    R = k - L
    if R <= 0:
        L = max(1, k // 2)
        R = k - L
    h = np.empty(k, dtype=np.float64)
    h[:L] = left_area / L
    h[L:] = (1.0 - left_area) / R
    return _normalize(h)


def _exponential_init(k, beta, reverse=False):
    k = int(k)
    if k <= 1:
        return np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, k)
    f = np.exp(float(beta) * x)
    if reverse:
        f = f[::-1]
    return _normalize(f)


def _random_block_init(k, m, alpha, rng):
    k = int(k)
    m = int(m)
    if k <= 0:
        return None
    if m <= 1:
        return np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64)
    if m > k:
        m = k

    cuts = [0]
    if m > 1:
        raw = np.sort(rng.integers(1, k, size=m - 1))
        for c in raw:
            if c > cuts[-1] and c < k:
                cuts.append(int(c))
    if cuts[-1] != k:
        cuts.append(k)
    if len(cuts) < 2:
        cuts = [0, k]

    while len(cuts) < m + 1:
        cuts = [0]
        step = max(1, k // (m + 1))
        for q in range(1, m):
            c = min(k - 1, min(k, cuts[-1] + step + int(rng.integers(0, step + 1))))
            if c > cuts[-1]:
                cuts.append(c)
        if cuts[-1] != k:
            cuts.append(k)

    mb = len(cuts) - 1
    areas = rng.dirichlet(np.full(mb, max(0.05, float(alpha))))
    h = np.zeros(k, dtype=np.float64)
    for j in range(mb):
        a0 = cuts[j]
        a1 = cuts[j + 1]
        length = a1 - a0
        if length > 0:
            h[a0:a1] = areas[j] / length
    return _normalize(h)


def _fourier_init(k, terms, rng, max_amp=1.0):
    k = int(k)
    if k <= 1:
        return np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, k)
    f = np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64)
    for _ in range(int(terms)):
        freq = int(rng.integers(1, 5))
        amp = float(rng.uniform(0.15, max_amp))
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * math.pi))
        f = f + amp * np.cos(2.0 * math.pi * freq * x + ph)
    f = np.maximum(f, 1e-4)
    return _normalize(f)


def _structured_sequence(k, kind, rng):
    k = int(k)
    if k <= 0:
        return None
    if kind == "tm":
        t = np.zeros(k, dtype=np.int8)
        s = np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64)
        for i in range(1, k):
            t[i] = int((t[i >> 1] ^ (i & 1)) & 1)
        s = 1.0 - 2.0 * t.astype(np.float64)
    elif kind == "rs":
        seq = np.array([1.0, -1.0])
        while seq.size < k:
            seq = np.concatenate((seq, -seq))
        s = seq[:k].astype(np.float64)
        if s.size < k:
            s = np.resize(s, k)
    elif kind == "chirp":
        x = np.arange(k, dtype=np.float64)
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * math.pi))
        s = np.sin(2.0 * math.pi * x * (x + 1.0) / (2.0 * float(k)) + ph)
        s = np.where(s >= 0.0, 1.0, -1.0)
    elif kind == "sin":
        x = np.arange(k, dtype=np.float64)
        freq = float(rng.uniform(0.7, 3.2))
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * math.pi))
        s = np.sin(2.0 * math.pi * freq * x / float(k) + ph)
    else:
        s = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=k)
    return s


def _structured_init(k, kind, amp, rng):
    s = _structured_sequence(k, kind, rng)
    if s is None:
        return None
    amp = float(amp)
    if kind != "sin":
        amp = min(1.0, max(0.0, amp))
    h = 1.0 + amp * s
    h = np.maximum(h, 1e-6)
    return _normalize(h)


def _resample_sequence(seq, n):
    a = _clean_array(seq)
    if a is None or a.size == 0:
        return None
    a = _normalize(a)
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return None
    if a.size == n:
        return a
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    b = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    b = np.where(np.isfinite(b), b, 0.0)
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    return _normalize(b)


def _upscale_h(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(n)
    if h.size == 0 or n <= 0:
        return None
    h = _normalize(h)
    k = h.size
    if n == k:
        return h
    if n < k:
        return _resample_sequence(h, n)
    base = n // k
    if base == 0:
        return _resample_sequence(h, n)
    extra = n - base * k
    out = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    pos = 0
    for i in range(k):
        length = base + (1 if i < extra else 0)
        if length > 0:
            out[pos:pos + length] = h[i] / length
            pos += length
    return _normalize(out)


def _add_candidate(lst, a):
    if a is None:
        return
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return
    a = _normalize(a)
    if np.all(np.isfinite(a)) and float(a.sum()) > 1e-12:
        lst.append(a)


def _initial_set_for_k(k, best_seq, rng, max_count=6):
    lst = []

    _add_candidate(lst, _two_slab(k, 0.5, 2.0 / 3.0))
    _add_candidate(lst, _two_slab(k, 0.5, 1.0 / 3.0))
    if best_seq is not None:
        _add_candidate(lst, _resample_sequence(best_seq, k))

    for m in (2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8):
        _add_candidate(lst, _random_block_init(k, m, float(rng.uniform(0.25, 2.0)), rng))

    for beta in (0.5, 1.0, 1.6, 2.2):
        _add_candidate(lst, _exponential_init(k, beta, False))
        _add_candidate(lst, _exponential_init(k, beta, True))

    for terms in (1, 2, 3):
        _add_candidate(lst, _fourier_init(k, terms, rng))

    for kind in ("tm", "rs", "chirp", "sin"):
        for amp in (0.3, 0.7, 1.0):
            _add_candidate(lst, _structured_init(k, kind, amp, rng))

    for conc in (0.1, 0.6, 2.0):
        _add_candidate(lst, rng.dirichlet(np.full(max(2, k), conc)))

    if len(lst) <= max_count:
        return lst

    keep = lst[:2]
    rest = lst[2:]
    need = max_count - len(keep)
    if need <= 0:
        return lst[:max_count]
    if len(rest) <= need:
        return keep + rest
    sel = rng.choice(len(rest), size=need, replace=False)
    return keep + [rest[i] for i in sel]


def _local_gradient_improve(h, deadline, rng):
    h = _normalize(h)
    n = h.size
    current = h.copy()
    current_score = _score_unit(current)
    best = current.copy()
    best_score = current_score
    logh = _log_normalize(current)

    eta = 0.22
    focus = 5.0
    no_improve = 0

    while _now() < deadline:
        if n == 0:
            break
        q = _convolve_self(current)
        mx = float(q.max())
        if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx <= 1e-300:
            break

        ratio = np.clip(q / mx, 0.0, 1.0)
        x = np.clip(ratio - 1.0, -1.0, 0.0)
        w = np.exp(focus * x)
        sw = float(w.sum())
        if sw <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(sw):
            break
        w = w / sw

        r = _corr_weights_h(w, current)
        r = np.where(np.isfinite(r), r, 0.0)
        pos = r[r > 0.0]
        if pos.size > 0:
            scale = float(np.percentile(pos, 80.0))
            if scale <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(scale):
                scale = float(np.max(pos))
        else:
            scale = 1.0

        newh = _natural_update(logh, r, max(scale, 1e-12), eta)
        new_score = _score_unit(newh)

        if np.isfinite(new_score) and new_score < current_score - 1e-12:
            current = newh
            current_score = new_score
            logh = _log_normalize(current)
            eta = min(2.0, eta * 1.04)
            no_improve = 0
            if new_score < best_score:
                best = current.copy()
                best_score = new_score
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve % 12 == 0:
                eta = max(0.02, eta * 0.85)
            if no_improve > 35:
                focus = min(28.0, focus * 1.18)
            if no_improve > 70:
                focus = max(1.0, focus * 0.82)
            if no_improve > 100:
                logh = _log_normalize(best)
                logh = logh + rng.normal(0.0, 0.22, size=n)
                cur = _natural_update(logh, r, max(scale, 1e-12), 0.0)
                cs = _score_unit(cur)
                if np.isfinite(cs) and cs <= best_score * 1.6:
                    current = cur
                    current_score = cs
                else:
                    current = best.copy()
                    current_score = best_score
                    logh = _log_normalize(current)
                no_improve = 0
                eta = 0.22
                focus = 5.0

    return best, best_score


def _block_perturb_improve(h, deadline, rng):
    h = _normalize(h)
    n = h.size
    current = h.copy()
    current_score = _score_unit(current)
    best = current.copy()
    best_score = current_score
    noise = 0.18
    no_improve = 0
    max_w = max(2, n // 18)

    while _now() < deadline:
        if n == 0:
            break
        width = int(rng.integers(1, max_w + 1))
        width = min(width, n)
        start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
        factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, noise)))
        nh = current.copy()
        nh[start:start + width] *= factor
        nh = _normalize(nh)
        ns = _score_unit(nh)
        if np.isfinite(ns) and ns < current_score - 1e-12:
            current = nh
            current_score = ns
            no_improve = 0
            noise = min(0.65, noise * 1.03)
            if ns < best_score:
                best = current.copy()
                best_score = ns
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            noise = max(0.035, noise * 0.94)
            if no_improve % 25 == 0:
                noise = min(0.5, noise * 1.6)
            if no_improve > 160:
                current = best.copy()
                current_score = best_score
                noise = 0.22
                no_improve = 0

    return best, best_score


def _pair_improve(h, deadline, rng):
    h = _normalize(h)
    n = h.size
    if n < 2:
        return h, _score_unit(h)
    current = h.copy()
    current_score = _score_unit(current)
    best = current.copy()
    best_score = current_score
    no_improve = 0
    taus = (0.04, 0.10, 0.22, 0.45)

    while _now() < deadline:
        i = int(rng.integers(n))
        j = int(rng.integers(n))
        if i == j:
            continue
        if current[i] >= current[j]:
            d, r = i, j
        else:
            d, r = j, i
        moved = False
        for tau in taus:
            val = current[d]
            if val <= 1e-300:
                continue
            amt = tau * val
            amt = min(amt, 0.9 * val)
            saved_d = current[d]
            saved_r = current[r]
            current[d] = saved_d - amt
            current[r] = saved_r + amt
            ns = _score_unit(current)
            if np.isfinite(ns) and ns < best_score - 1e-12:
                best = current.copy()
                best_score = ns
                current_score = ns
                moved = True
                no_improve = 0
                break
            else:
                current[d] = saved_d
                current[r] = saved_r
        if not moved:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 4000:
                current = best.copy()
                current_score = best_score
                no_improve = 0

    return best, best_score


def _optimize_k(h, deadline, rng):
    if h is None:
        return None, float("inf")
    h = _normalize(h)
    best = h
    best_score = _score_unit(h)
    n = h.size

    t0 = _now()
    t_grad = min(deadline, t0 + max(1.0, (deadline - t0) * 0.58))
    h1, s1 = _local_gradient_improve(h, t_grad, rng)
    if h1 is not None and np.isfinite(s1) and s1 < best_score:
        best = h1
        best_score = s1

    t1 = _now()
    if t1 < deadline and n <= 6000:
        t_block = min(deadline, t1 + max(0.5, (deadline - t1) * 0.30))
        h2, s2 = _block_perturb_improve(best, t_block, rng)
        if h2 is not None and np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score:
            best = h2
            best_score = s2

    t2 = _now()
    if t2 < deadline and n <= 260:
        h3, s3 = _pair_improve(best, t2 + max(0.5, deadline - t2), rng)
        if h3 is not None and np.isfinite(s3) and s3 < best_score:
            best = h3
            best_score = s3

    return best, best_score


def _get_parent_sequence():
    try:
        parent = height_sequence_1  # noqa: F821
    except Exception:
        parent = None
    return _clean_array(parent)


def _finalize_candidates(best_seq, parent, final_deadline):
    cands = []

    _add_candidate(cands, best_seq)
    _add_candidate(cands, parent)
    _add_candidate(cands, _two_slab(4, 0.5, 2.0 / 3.0))
    _add_candidate(cands, _two_slab(64, 0.5, 2.0 / 3.0))
    _add_candidate(cands, _two_slab(5582, 0.5, 2.0 / 3.0))

    if best_seq is not None:
        base = int(best_seq.size)
        tries = {base}
        for mult in (2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32):
            N = base * mult
            if 8 <= N <= 8000:
                tries.add(N)
        if 8 <= 5582:
            tries.add(5582)
        if 256 <= base <= 8000:
            tries.add(base)

        for N in sorted(tries):
            if N <= 1 or N > 8000:
                continue
            if N >= base:
                cand = _upscale_h(best_seq, N)
            else:
                cand = _resample_sequence(best_seq, N)
            _add_candidate(cands, cand)

    selected = None
    selected_score = float("inf")
    exact_scores = []

    for cand in cands:
        cand = _clean_array(cand)
        if cand is None or cand.size == 0:
            continue
        cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(cand.sum())
        if s <= 1e-12:
            continue
        cand = cand / s
        sc = _score_raw(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < selected_score:
            selected_score = sc
            selected = cand

    if selected is not None:
        selected_list = [float(x) for x in selected]
        try:
            ext = float(evaluate_sequence(selected_list))  # noqa: F821
        except Exception:
            ext = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(ext):
            selected_score = ext
            exact_scores.append((ext, selected))
        else:
            exact_scores.append((selected_score, selected))
    else:
        fallback = _two_slab(64, 0.5, 2.0 / 3.0).tolist()
        exact_scores.append((float("inf"), fallback))

    best_score_final = float("inf")
    best_cand = None
    exact_scores.sort(key=lambda p: p[0])
    for sc, cand in exact_scores[:5]:
        if cand is None:
            continue
        lst = [float(x) for x in cand]
        try:
            esc = float(evaluate_sequence(lst))  # noqa: F821
        except Exception:
            esc = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(esc) and esc < best_score_final:
            best_score_final = esc
            best_cand = lst

    if best_cand is None:
        best_cand = fallback
    return best_cand


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    if seed is None:
        seed = 42
    try:
        seed = int(seed)
    except Exception:
        seed = 42

    try:
        budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget_s = 2400.0

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start_t = _now()
    search_deadline = start_t + max(60.0, budget_s - 25.0)

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    def maybe_update(h, score):
        nonlocal best_seq, best_score
        if h is None:
            return
        h = _normalize(h)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(h)):
            return
        sc = _score_unit(h)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = h

    # Solid safe baseline / parent.
    parent = _get_parent_sequence()

    for k0 in (4, 16, 64):
        maybe_update(_two_slab(k0, 0.5, 2.0 / 3.0), 0.0)

    if parent is not None:
        maybe_update(parent, 0.0)

    base_ks = [8, 12, 16, 22, 32, 44, 64, 96, 128, 192, 256, 384, 512, 768, 1024, 1536, 2048]
    if parent is not None:
        psize = int(parent.size)
        if 8 <= psize <= 2048:
            if psize not in base_ks:
                base_ks.append(psize)
                base_ks.sort()
        for kk in (max(8, psize // 4), max(8, psize // 2), min(2048, psize * 2)):
            if 8 <= kk <= 2048 and kk not in base_ks:
                base_ks.append(kk)
        base_ks.sort()

    pass_no = 0
    while _now() < search_deadline - 40.0:
        pass_no += 1

        if pass_no == 1:
            run_sec = 8.0
            count = 4
            ks = base_ks
        elif pass_no == 2:
            run_sec = 20.0
            count = 5
            ks = [k for k in base_ks if k <= 2048]
        else:
            run_sec = 30.0
            count = 5
            ks = [k for k in base_ks if 16 <= k <= 2048]

        if not ks:
            ks = [64, 128, 256]

        for k in ks:
            if _now() >= search_deadline - 40.0:
                break
            inits = _initial_set_for_k(k, best_seq, rng, count)
            if not inits:
                continue
            for h0 in inits:
                if _now() >= search_deadline - 40.0:
                    break
                t_end = min(search_deadline - 5.0, _now() + run_sec)
                h_opt, sc_opt = _optimize_k(h0, t_end, rng)
                if h_opt is not None and np.isfinite(sc_opt):
                    maybe_update(h_opt, sc_opt)

    # Final polish on the best sequence itself.
    if best_seq is not None and _now() < search_deadline - 10.0:
        h_opt, sc_opt = _optimize_k(best_seq, search_deadline - 5.0, rng)
        if h_opt is not None and np.isfinite(sc_opt):
            maybe_update(h_opt, sc_opt)

    final_deadline = _now() + max(0.0, budget_s - 2.0)
    ans = _finalize_candidates(best_seq, parent, final_deadline)

    # Last safety: ensure list type, float type, nonnegative, finite.
    out = []
    for x in ans:
        try:
            y = float(x)
        except Exception:
            y = 0.0
        if not np.isfinite(y):
            y = 0.0
        out.append(max(0.0, min(1000.0, y)))
    if not out:
        out = [1.0]
    return out
```
